2.  When  he  was  standihg,  he  did  not  occupy  the  middle  of  the

3.  When  be  was  passing  the  vacant  place  of  the  prince,  his  counte-
nance appeared  to  change,  and  his  legs  to  bend  under  him,  and  his

4.  He  ascended  the  reception  hall,  holding  up  his  robe  with  both
his  hands,  and  his  body  bent ; holding  in  his  breath  also,  as  if  he
dared  not  breathe.

5.  When  he  came  out  from  the  audience,  as  soon  as  he  had  de-
scended one  step,  he  beigati  to  relax  his  countenance,  and  had  a
satisfied  look.  When  he  had  got  to  the  bottom  of  the  steps,  lie
advanced  rapidly  to  his  place,  with  his  arms  like  wihgs,  and  on
